[Objective]:
Critic_Check

[Prompt]:
Role: you are an expert urban data analyst and human behavior specialist.
Task: your goal is to critically evaluate a person's daily activity trajectory for semantic and logical plausibility based on common sense and typical urban life patterns.
you will be given the date and a sequence of activities. Each activity is a string with a location/action and a timestamp.
**Input Data:**
- Date: 2019-10-28
- Trajectory: ['Lottery Retailer#41 at 08:00', 'Rock Club#144 at 08:10', 'Electronics Store#676 at 17:30', 'Multiplex#283 at 22:00', 'Supermarket#1773 at 22:30']
**Critical Thinking Checklist:**
1.  **Temporal Logic (Time-based Sense):**
    -   **Opening Hours:** Is the venue (e.g., bank, store, educational institution) likely to be open at the specified time? Consider the day of the week (weekday/weekend/holiday) implied by the date.
    -   **Activity-Time Congruence:** Does the activity make sense at that time of day? (e.g., visiting a nightclub at 9 AM is unusual).
    -   **Duration Plausibility:** Is the time elapsed between consecutive events reasonable for the implied activity? For example, a 5-hour gap for "Travel to work" within the same city is highly suspicious. Pay close attention to long durations covered by `(Implied)` activities.
2.  **Sequential & Causal Logic (Order of Events):**
    -   **Common Sense Flow:** Does the sequence of activities follow a logical progression? (e.g., People usually go grocery shopping before returning home, not after).
    -   **Daily Rhythm:** Does the trajectory represent a coherent part of a day? Does it start and end in a plausible manner (e.g., most daily trajectories start or end at a residential location, even if not explicitly stated)?
**Output Format:**
you MUST respond with a JSON object. Do not add any text before or after the JSON.
- If the plan is reasonable, output exactly: "Pass"
- If there are logical errors, briefly explain the specific error in one sentence.
**Examples:**
-   **Example 1 (Plausible):**
    -   Date: 2026-01-05 (Monday)
    -   Trajectory: ["Home at 08:00", "Office#123 at 09:00", "Restaurant#45 at 12:15", "Office#123 at 13:30", "Home at 18:00"]
    -   Expected Output: `"Pass"`
-   **Example 2 (Temporal Flaw):**
    -   Date: 2026-01-06 (Tuesday)
    -   Trajectory: ["Restaurant#50 at 22:00", "Bank#789 at 23:00"]
    -   Expected Output: `"Banks are typically closed at 23:00 on a weekday."`
Now, analyze the provided input data and generate your response.

[Result]:
```json
{
  "Date": "2019-10-28",
  "Trajectory": [
    "Lottery Retailer#41 at 08:00",
    "Rock Club#144 at 08:10",
    "Electronics Store#676 at 17:30",
    "Multiplex#283 at 22:00",
    "Supermarket#1773 at 22:30"
  ],
  "Analysis": "Visiting a Rock Club at 08:10 in the morning is highly unusual as these venues are typically closed during standard business hours."
}
```

Task: your goal is to critically evaluate a person's daily activity trajectory for semantic and logical plausibility based on common sense and typical urban life patterns.
you will be given the date and a sequence of activities. Each activity is a string with a location/action and a timestamp.
**Input Data:**
- Date: 2019-10-30
- Trajectory: ['Lottery Retailer#41 at 08:00', 'Rock Club#144 at 08:10', 'Platform#123 at 09:00', 'Electronics Store#676 at 17:30', 'Platform#41 at 19:00', 'Multiplex#283 at 21:00', 'Supermarket#1773 at 22:30']
**Critical Thinking Checklist:**
1.  **Temporal Logic (Time-based Sense):**
    -   **Opening Hours:** Is the venue (e.g., bank, store, educational institution) likely to be open at the specified time? Consider the day of the week (weekday/weekend/holiday) implied by the date.
    -   **Activity-Time Congruence:** Does the activity make sense at that time of day? (e.g., visiting a nightclub at 9 AM is unusual).
    -   **Duration Plausibility:** Is the time elapsed between consecutive events reasonable for the implied activity? For example, a 5-hour gap for "Travel to work" within the same city is highly suspicious. Pay close attention to long durations covered by `(Implied)` activities.
2.  **Sequential & Causal Logic (Order of Events):**
    -   **Common Sense Flow:** Does the sequence of activities follow a logical progression? (e.g., People usually go grocery shopping before returning home, not after).
    -   **Daily Rhythm:** Does the trajectory represent a coherent part of a day? Does it start and end in a plausible manner (e.g., most daily trajectories start or end at a residential location, even if not explicitly stated)?

you MUST respond with a JSON object. Do not add any text before or after the JSON.
- If the plan is reasonable, output exactly: "Pass"
- If there are logical errors, briefly explain the specific error in one sentence.

    -   **Daily Rhythm:** Does the trajectory represent a coherent part of a day? Does it start and end in a plausible manner (e.g., most daily trajectories start or end at a residential location, even if not explicitly stated)?

Role: you are an expert urban data analyst and human behavior specialist.
Task: your goal is to critically evaluate a person's daily activity trajectory for semantic and logical plausibility based on common sense and typical urban life patterns.
you will be given the date and a sequence of activities. Each activity is a string with a location/action and a timestamp.
**Input Data:**
- Date: 2019-10-31
- Trajectory: ['Lottery Retailer#41 at 08:00', 'Rock Club#144 at 08:10', 'Rock Club#123 at 12:30', 'Electronics Store#676 at 17:30', 'Multiplex#283 at 19:30', 'Supermarket#1773 at 22:30']
**Critical Thinking Checklist:**
1.  **Temporal Logic (Time-based Sense):**
    -   **Opening Hours:** Is the venue (e.g., bank, store, educational institution) likely to be open at the specified time? Consider the day of the week (weekday/weekend/holiday) implied by the date.
    -   **Activity-Time Congruence:** Does the activity make sense at that time of day? (e.g., visiting a nightclub at 9 AM is unusual).
    -   **Duration Plausibility:** Is the time elapsed between consecutive events reasonable for the implied activity? For example, a 5-hour gap for "Travel to work" within the same city is highly suspicious. Pay close attention to long durations covered by `(Implied)` activities.
2.  **Sequential & Causal Logic (Order of Events):**
    -   **Common Sense Flow:** Does the sequence of activities follow a logical progression? (e.g., People usually go grocery shopping before returning home, not after).
    -   **Daily Rhythm:** Does the trajectory represent a coherent part of a day? Does it start and end in a plausible manner (e.g., most daily trajectories start or end at a residential location, even if not explicitly stated)?
**Output Format:**
you MUST respond with a JSON object. Do not add any text before or after the JSON.
- If the plan is reasonable, output exactly: "Pass"
- If there are logical errors, briefly explain the specific error in one sentence.
**Examples:**
-   **Example 1 (Plausible):**
    -   Date: 2026-01-05 (Monday)
    -   Trajectory: ["Home at 08:00", "Office#123 at 09:00", "Restaurant#45 at 12:15", "Office#123 at 13:30", "Home at 18:00"]
    -   Expected Output: `"Pass"`
-   **Example 2 (Temporal Flaw):**
    -   Date: 2026-01-06 (Tuesday)
    -   Trajectory: ["Restaurant#50 at 22:00", "Bank#789 at 23:00"]

Now, analyze the provided input data and generate your response.

[Result]:

[Objective]:
Critic_Check

[Prompt]:
Role: you are an expert urban data analyst and human behavior specialist.
Task: your goal is to critically evaluate a person's daily activity trajectory for semantic and logical plausibility based on common sense and typical urban life patterns.
you will be given the date and a sequence of activities. Each activity is a string with a location/action and a timestamp.
**Input Data:**
- Date: 2019-11-04
- Trajectory: ['Lottery Retailer#41 at 08:00', 'Rock Club#144 at 08:10', 'Electronics Store#676 at 17:30', 'Discount Store#1414 at 19:30', 'Convenience Store#3786 at 21:00', 'Landmark#612 at 22:00', 'Multiplex#283 at 22:45']
**Critical Thinking Checklist:**
1.  **Temporal Logic (Time-based Sense):**
    -   **Opening Hours:** Is the venue (e.g., bank, store, educational institution) likely to be open at the specified time? Consider the day of the week (weekday/weekend/holiday) implied by the date.
    -   **Activity-Time Congruence:** Does the activity make sense at that time of day? (e.g., visiting a nightclub at 9 AM is unusual).
    -   **Duration Plausibility:** Is the time elapsed between consecutive events reasonable for the implied activity? For example, a 5-hour gap for "Travel to work" within the same city is highly suspicious. Pay close attention to long durations covered by `(Implied)` activities.
2.  **Sequential & Causal Logic (Order of Events):**
    -   **Common Sense Flow:** Does the sequence of activities follow a logical progression? (e.g., People usually go grocery shopping before returning home, not after).
    -   **Daily Rhythm:** Does the trajectory represent a coherent part of a day? Does it start and end in a plausible manner (e.g., most daily trajectories start or end at a residential location, even if not explicitly stated)?
**Output Format:**
you MUST respond with a JSON object. Do not add any text before or after the JSON.
- If the plan is reasonable, output exactly: "Pass"
- If there are logical errors, briefly explain the specific error in one sentence.
**Examples:**
-   **Example 1 (Plausible):**
    -   Date: 2026-01-05 (Monday)
    -   Trajectory: ["Home at 08:00", "Office#123 at 09:00", "Restaurant#45 at 12:15", "Office#123 at 13:30", "Home at 18:00"]
    -   Expected Output: `"Pass"`
-   **Example 2 (Temporal Flaw):**
    -   Date: 2026-01-06 (Tuesday)
    -   Trajectory: ["Restaurant#50 at 22:00", "Bank#789 at 23:00"]
    -   Expected Output: `"Banks are typically closed at 23:00 on a weekday."`
Now, analyze the provided input data and generate your response.

[Result]:
```json
{
  "analysis": "The trajectory shows a visit to a Lottery Retailer followed immediately by a visit to a Rock Club 10 minutes later, which is highly improbable given the typical opening hours of a rock club and the required travel time between locations.",
  "result": "The sequence of visiting a Lottery Retailer at 08:00 and then a Rock Club at 08:10 is temporally and logically implausible as rock clubs are rarely open that early."
}
```

[Objective]:
Critic_Check

[Prompt]:
Role: you are an expert urban data analyst and human behavior specialist.
Task: your goal is to critically evaluate a person's daily activity trajectory for semantic and logical plausibility based on common sense and typical urban life patterns.
you will be given the date and a sequence of activities. Each activity is a string with a location/action and a timestamp.
**Input Data:**
- Date: 2019-11-08
- Trajectory: ['Lottery Retailer#41 at 08:00', 'Rock Club#144 at 08:10', 'Discount Store#1414 at 09:00', 'Music School#24 at 10:30', 'Convention Center#415 at 12:00', 'Ramen Restaurant#1691 at 13:30', 'Electronics Store#676 at 17:30', 'Platform#41 at 19:00', 'Multiplex#283 at 20:30', 'Supermarket#1773 at 22:30']
**Critical Thinking Checklist:**
1.  **Temporal Logic (Time-based Sense):**
    -   **Opening Hours:** Is the venue (e.g., bank, store, educational institution) likely to be open at the specified time? Consider the day of the week (weekday/weekend/holiday) implied by the date.
    -   **Activity-Time Congruence:** Does the activity make sense at that time of day? (e.g., visiting a nightclub at 9 AM is unusual).
    -   **Duration Plausibility:** Is the time elapsed between consecutive events reasonable for the implied activity? For example, a 5-hour gap for "Travel to work" within the same city is highly suspicious. Pay close attention to long durations covered by `(Implied)` activities.
2.  **Sequential & Causal Logic (Order of Events):**
    -   **Common Sense Flow:** Does the sequence of activities follow a logical progression? (e.g., People usually go grocery shopping before returning home, not after).
    -   **Daily Rhythm:** Does the trajectory represent a coherent part of a day? Does it start and end in a plausible manner (e.g., most daily trajectories start or end at a residential location, even if not explicitly stated)?
**Output Format:**
you MUST respond with a JSON object. Do not add any text before or after the JSON.
- If the plan is reasonable, output exactly: "Pass"
- If there are logical errors, briefly explain the specific error in one sentence.
**Examples:**
-   **Example 1 (Plausible):**
    -   Date: 2026-01-05 (Monday)
    -   Trajectory: ["Home at 08:00", "Office#123 at 09:00", "Restaurant#45 at 12:15", "Office#123 at 13:30", "Home at 18:00"]
    -   Expected Output: `"Pass"`
-   **Example 2 (Temporal Flaw):**
    -   Date: 2026-01-06 (Tuesday)
    -   Trajectory: ["Restaurant#50 at 22:00", "Bank#789 at 23:00"]
    -   Expected Output: `"Banks are typically closed at 23:00 on a weekday."`
Now, analyze the provided input data and generate your response.

[Result]:
```json
{
  "analysis": "The sequence is highly implausible due to the immediate transition from a 'Lottery Retailer' at 08:00 to a 'Rock Club' at 08:10, which are unlikely to be geographically adjacent or relevant to a typical morning routine, followed by an immediate 50-minute gap before a 'Discount Store' visit."
}
```
==================== END RECORD ====================

    -   **Opening Hours:** Is the venue (e.g., bank, store, educational institution) likely to be open at the specified time? Consider the day of the week (weekday/weekend/holiday) implied by the date.

    -   **Opening Hours:** Is the venue (e.g., bank, store, educational institution) likely to be open at the specified time? Consider the day of the week (weekday/weekend/holiday) implied by the date.
    -   **Activity-Time Congruence:** Does the activity make sense at that time of day? (e.g., visiting a nightclub at 9 AM is unusual).
    -   **Duration Plausibility:** Is the time elapsed between consecutive events reasonable for the implied activity? For example, a 5-hour gap for "Travel to work" within the same city is highly suspicious. Pay close attention to long durations covered by `(Implied)` activities.
2.  **Sequential & Causal Logic (Order of Events):**
    -   **Common Sense Flow:** Does the sequence of activities follow a logical progression? (e.g., People usually go grocery shopping before returning home, not after).
    -   **Daily Rhythm:** Does the trajectory represent a coherent part of a day? Does it start and end in a plausible manner (e.g., most daily trajectories start or end at a residential location, even if not explicitly stated)?

- If there are logical errors, briefly explain the specific error in one sentence.

[Objective]:
Critic_Check

[Prompt]:
Role: you are an expert urban data analyst and human behavior specialist.
Task: your goal is to critically evaluate a person's daily activity trajectory for semantic and logical plausibility based on common sense and typical urban life patterns.
you will be given the date and a sequence of activities. Each activity is a string with a location/action and a timestamp.
**Input Data:**
- Date: 2019-11-14
- Trajectory: ['Lottery Retailer#41 at 08:00', 'Rock Club#144 at 08:10', 'Electronics Store#676 at 17:30', 'Multiplex#283 at 21:00', 'Supermarket#1773 at 22:30']
**Critical Thinking Checklist:**
1.  **Temporal Logic (Time-based Sense):**
    -   **Opening Hours:** Is the venue (e.g., bank, store, educational institution) likely to be open at the specified time? Consider the day of the week (weekday/weekend/holiday) implied by the date.
    -   **Activity-Time Congruence:** Does the activity make sense at that time of day? (e.g., visiting a nightclub at 9 AM is unusual).
    -   **Duration Plausibility:** Is the time elapsed between consecutive events reasonable for the implied activity? For example, a 5-hour gap for "Travel to work" within the same city is highly suspicious. Pay close attention to long durations covered by `(Implied)` activities.
2.  **Sequential & Causal Logic (Order of Events):**
    -   **Common Sense Flow:** Does the sequence of activities follow a logical progression? (e.g., People usually go grocery shopping before returning home, not after).
    -   **Daily Rhythm:** Does the trajectory represent a coherent part of a day? Does it start and end in a plausible manner (e.g., most daily trajectories start or end at a residential location, even if not explicitly stated)?
**Output Format:**
you MUST respond with a JSON object. Do not add any text before or after the JSON.
- If the plan is reasonable, output exactly: "Pass"
- If there are logical errors, briefly explain the specific error in one sentence.
**Examples:**
-   **Example 1 (Plausible):**
    -   Date: 2026-01-05 (Monday)
    -   Trajectory: ["Home at 08:00", "Office#123 at 09:00", "Restaurant#45 at 12:15", "Office#123 at 13:30", "Home at 18:00"]
    -   Expected Output: `"Pass"`
-   **Example 2 (Temporal Flaw):**
    -   Date: 2026-01-06 (Tuesday)
    -   Trajectory: ["Restaurant#50 at 22:00", "Bank#789 at 23:00"]
    -   Expected Output: `"Banks are typically closed at 23:00 on a weekday."`
Now, analyze the provided input data and generate your response.

[Result]:
```json
{
  "analysis": "The sequence of visiting a Lottery Retailer followed immediately by a Rock Club at 08:00 and 08:10 respectively is highly implausible, as most retail and entertainment venues are not open that early in the morning."
}
```

[Prompt]:
Role: you are an expert urban data analyst and human behavior specialist.
Task: your goal is to critically evaluate a person's daily activity trajectory for semantic and logical plausibility based on common sense and typical urban life patterns.
you will be given the date and a sequence of activities. Each activity is a string with a location/action and a timestamp.
**Input Data:**
- Date: 2019-11-20
- Trajectory: ['Lottery Retailer#41 at 08:00', 'Rock Club#144 at 08:10', 'Electronics Store#676 at 17:30', 'Discount Store#1414 at 20:00', 'Multiplex#283 at 22:00']
**Critical Thinking Checklist:**
1.  **Temporal Logic (Time-based Sense):**
    -   **Opening Hours:** Is the venue (e.g., bank, store, educational institution) likely to be open at the specified time? Consider the day of the week (weekday/weekend/holiday) implied by the date.
    -   **Activity-Time Congruence:** Does the activity make sense at that time of day? (e.g., visiting a nightclub at 9 AM is unusual).
    -   **Duration Plausibility:** Is the time elapsed between consecutive events reasonable for the implied activity? For example, a 5-hour gap for "Travel to work" within the same city is highly suspicious. Pay close attention to long durations covered by `(Implied)` activities.
2.  **Sequential & Causal Logic (Order of Events):**
    -   **Common Sense Flow:** Does the sequence of activities follow a logical progression? (e.g., People usually go grocery shopping before returning home, not after).
    -   **Daily Rhythm:** Does the trajectory represent a coherent part of a day? Does it start and end in a plausible manner (e.g., most daily trajectories start or end at a residential location, even if not explicitly stated)?
**Output Format:**
you MUST respond with a JSON object. Do not add any text before or after the JSON.
- If the plan is reasonable, output exactly: "Pass"
- If there are logical errors, briefly explain the specific error in one sentence.
**Examples:**
-   **Example 1 (Plausible):**
    -   Date: 2026-01-05 (Monday)
    -   Trajectory: ["Home at 08:00", "Office#123 at 09:00", "Restaurant#45 at 12:15", "Office#123 at 13:30", "Home at 18:00"]
    -   Expected Output: `"Pass"`
-   **Example 2 (Temporal Flaw):**
    -   Date: 2026-01-06 (Tuesday)
    -   Trajectory: ["Restaurant#50 at 22:00", "Bank#789 at 23:00"]
    -   Expected Output: `"Banks are typically closed at 23:00 on a weekday."`
Now, analyze the provided input data and generate your response.

[Result]:
```json
{
  "Critique": "Visiting a Rock Club immediately after visiting a Lottery Retailer at 08:10 AM is highly implausible as rock clubs are rarely open that early in the morning."
}
```

you will be given the date and a sequence of activities. Each activity is a string with a location/action and a timestamp.
**Input Data:**
- Date: 2019-11-20
- Trajectory: ['Lottery Retailer#41 at 08:00', 'Rock Club#144 at 08:10', 'Electronics Store#676 at 17:30', 'Discount Store#130 at 20:00', 'Multiplex#283 at 22:00']
**Critical Thinking Checklist:**
1.  **Temporal Logic (Time-based Sense):**
    -   **Opening Hours:** Is the venue (e.g., bank, store, educational institution) likely to be open at the specified time? Consider the day of the week (weekday/weekend/holiday) implied by the date.
    -   **Activity-Time Congruence:** Does the activity make sense at that time of day? (e.g., visiting a nightclub at 9 AM is unusual).
    -   **Duration Plausibility:** Is the time elapsed between consecutive events reasonable for the implied activity? For example, a 5-hour gap for "Travel to work" within the same city is highly suspicious. Pay close attention to long durations covered by `(Implied)` activities.
2.  **Sequential & Causal Logic (Order of Events):**
    -   **Common Sense Flow:** Does the sequence of activities follow a logical progression? (e.g., People usually go grocery shopping before returning home, not after).
    -   **Daily Rhythm:** Does the trajectory represent a coherent part of a day? Does it start and end in a plausible manner (e.g., most daily trajectories start or end at a residential location, even if not explicitly stated)?
**Output Format:**
you MUST respond with a JSON object. Do not add any text before or after the JSON.
- If the plan is reasonable, output exactly: "Pass"
- If there are logical errors, briefly explain the specific error in one sentence.
**Examples:**
-   **Example 1 (Plausible):**
    -   Date: 2026-01-05 (Monday)
    -   Trajectory: ["Home at 08:00", "Office#123 at 09:00", "Restaurant#45 at 12:15", "Office#123 at 13:30", "Home at 18:00"]
    -   Expected Output: `"Pass"`
-   **Example 2 (Temporal Flaw):**
    -   Date: 2026-01-06 (Tuesday)
    -   Trajectory: ["Restaurant#50 at 22:00", "Bank#789 at 23:00"]
    -   Expected Output: `"Banks are typically closed at 23:00 on a weekday."`
Now, analyze the provided input data and generate your response.

[Result]:
```json
{
  "evaluation": "The sequence of visiting a Lottery Retailer followed immediately by a Rock Club ten minutes later is highly implausible, as rock clubs are generally not open or active at 8:10 AM on a Wednesday."
}
```

you will be given the date and a sequence of activities. Each activity is a string with a location/action and a timestamp.
**Input Data:**
- Date: 2019-11-21
- Trajectory: ['Lottery Retailer#41 at 08:00', 'Rock Club#144 at 08:10', 'Electronics Store#676 at 17:30', 'Multiplex#283 at 21:00']
**Critical Thinking Checklist:**
1.  **Temporal Logic (Time-based Sense):**
    -   **Opening Hours:** Is the venue (e.g., bank, store, educational institution) likely to be open at the specified time? Consider the day of the week (weekday/weekend/holiday) implied by the date.
    -   **Activity-Time Congruence:** Does the activity make sense at that time of day? (e.g., visiting a nightclub at 9 AM is unusual).
    -   **Duration Plausibility:** Is the time elapsed between consecutive events reasonable for the implied activity? For example, a 5-hour gap for "Travel to work" within the same city is highly suspicious. Pay close attention to long durations covered by `(Implied)` activities.
2.  **Sequential & Causal Logic (Order of Events):**
    -   **Common Sense Flow:** Does the sequence of activities follow a logical progression? (e.g., People usually go grocery shopping before returning home, not after).
    -   **Daily Rhythm:** Does the trajectory represent a coherent part of a day? Does it start and end in a plausible manner (e.g., most daily trajectories start or end at a residential location, even if not explicitly stated)?

you MUST respond with a JSON object. Do not add any text before or after the JSON.
- If the plan is reasonable, output exactly: "Pass"
- If there are logical errors, briefly explain the specific error in one sentence.
